# Decisions

Each item gives the decision, the evidence, and what would reopen it. Numbers are never reused, and 3, 28, 46, 51, 60, 87, 88, 132, 135 and 213 are not in use. Spikes 1 to 8 ran on Claude Code 2.1.296, the spikes of decisions 74 to 85 and 100 to 103 on 2.1.304, those of decisions 118 to 134 on 2.1.310, and that of decision 315 on 2.1.325.

1. **A self-contained repository.** Every file is written for this suite. No file imports history, links to another project, maps names, records an acceptance run made elsewhere, or notes what came before. Lessons enter the requirements as rules with no source line. Reopens: never; this is the premise of the repository.
2. **Plugin names `rules`, `loop`, `audit`, `repo-docs`.** They give `/rules`, `/loop:run`, `/audit:run` and `/repo-docs`. Spike 8 confirmed that a skill named like its plugin answers to the bare `/rules` as well as `/rules:rules` on 2.1.296; the documentation describes only the namespaced form, so the README shows both. The spike says nothing about a name a bundled skill owns; see decision 35 for `loop`. Reopens: a host release where the bare form stops working, which the acceptance item for `/rules` would catch.
4. **Both house-rule edits ship.** The two Working items in requirements 19 and 20. Removing any stop was rejected. Reopens: the owner's say-so.
5. **No effort tiers in the loop.** One default run budget of 240 minutes, the risk floor, and a prose effort hint. A tier would choose a model per step, and the suite has one model per role, so a tier could only set the budget, which the hint and `.reimagine.json` already do. Reopens: field runs that need a budget the hint and the configuration cannot express.
6. **State directory `.reimagine/`, namespaced per plugin.** The loop writes `.reimagine/loop/<branch-slug>/` and adds `.reimagine/` to `.git/info/exclude`; the audit writes `.reimagine/audit/<run-id>/` only when `.reimagine/` is already ignored, and otherwise falls back to the plugin data directory, because the audit never writes under `.git/`. The specification named `.reimagine/<branch-slug>/`; the extra level keeps a branch slug from colliding with an audit run id. Repository configuration is `.reimagine.json`. Reopens: a host feature that gives plugins a per-repository ignored directory. Decision 326 adds an untracked `.reimagine.json` to the same exclude file.
7. **Azure DevOps pull request publishing is out of scope.** Reads and discussion comments go through an MCP tool or `az`; a run on an Azure DevOps remote ends `prepared` with the push command. Reopens: a tested `az repos pr create` path with an acceptance item.
8. **No attribution hook.** Spike 5: with the attribution setting off, three of three commit runs on 2.1.296 (opus, sonnet, opus) produced no trailer; with it on, the model added one and said the session setup told it to. A hook denying a commit that carries a trailer while the setting is off would guard against a behavior that did not occur. Requirement 11 keeps the rule that the loop never types a trailer. Reopens: a trailer appearing in the field with attribution off. Decision 316 denies a commit whose trailer names a string the user listed, whatever the setting; it removes nothing.
9. **No `userConfig` anywhere.** Spike 4: a boolean option with `default: true`, referenced as `${user_config.flag}` in a hook command, failed every run while unset with `Plugin option "flag" isn't set. Open /plugin manage to configure it`. The default was not substituted. Options are expressed in `.reimagine.json` or in prose instead. Reopens: a host release that substitutes defaults, confirmed by rerunning the spike.
10. **Forbidden names are enforced from outside the repository.** A list of forbidden names inside the repository would itself be a file holding them. Lint reads `LINT_DENY_TOKENS` from the environment when set and fails on any tracked file containing a listed token; CI leaves it unset and the implementing session sets it locally before every commit. Reopens: never; the need ends with 0.1.0.
11. **Fixture literals are observed, not computed.** The audit script suites compare bytes and shas that embed strings (`reimagine-handoff: 1`, `# audit report: `, `audit:auditor`, the `working-tree.sh` commit identity, the fixture handoff blob). Every literal that depends on such a string is produced by running the fixture builder once and pasting the observed value, in its own commit, so the diff shows which values follow from which strings. Reopens: never.
12. **`jq` is an optional prerequisite of the audit.** Three scripts need it; Git for Windows does not bundle it; a Node rewrite would add code without a suite behind it. The README names it and stage 1 warns when it is missing. The repo-docs hook stays free of it. Reopens: `jq` installs proving a recurring support cost, which would justify the rewrite with its own suite.
13. **The credential scan belongs to the loop.** The specification places the scan on ticket text before it is written to a run directory. The loop writes ticket text into its record, so it scans. The audit redirects forge reads to files without passing them through the model and keeps its run directory ignored; adding a scan there would be new scope. Reopens: a field run where audit run-directory contents leak into something published.
14. **Trigger phrases live in the skill description.** Spike 1: a description naming "reimagine loop" and "run the loop" invoked the skill in two of two opus runs; a terse description without the phrase also invoked a skill named `loop` once; a negative control did not. `when_to_use` is not used because the spike did not cover it and the host appends it to the description anyway. Reopens: a trigger miss in acceptance.
15. **Reviewer fallback is written into the skill.** Spike 2: `fable` and `opus` are accepted agent model names on this account; an unknown name fails the Agent call with an API 404 that the orchestrator sees. The skill retries once at `opus` on a model or tool error, records the swap, and stays swapped. Reopens: a host feature that falls back by itself. Amended 2026-11-05: the reviewer runs at `opus` and the retry is at the `fallback-reviewer` model, `sonnet` by default, with the swap of decision 182.
16. **`steps.json` is the loop's completion record.** Parsing prose for resume is fragile; a JSON entry written `complete` only after its outputs exist is a reliable single source, and the audit's `stages.json` follows the same pattern. `run.md` stays the narrative. Reopens: never.
17. **The diff review has a defined request.** Requirement 66 states what a diff reviewer receives: plan, inputs, acceptance criteria, dispositions, and the diff read from the merge-base after `git add -N`. No credential scan runs on the diff: it stays with the session's own model provider. Reopens: a finding that a reviewer subagent surfaced a credential into the record.
18. **Review calls get a 30-minute budget.** A live run's final review over 37 files took 20 minutes 42 seconds and blocked at a 20-minute budget with no path to resume. Review passes have their own `review` budget of 30 minutes, and the idempotent record lets a blocked step 5 be rerun. Reopens: budget data from the first ten field runs.
19. **A missing `code-review` skill does not block a higher-risk run.** An Opus subagent reviewing a patch file serves worktree and additional-repository diffs; the same path serves when the skill is not listed. Reopens: never.
20. **The loop edits only pull request bodies it wrote.** A body belongs to whoever wrote it. A pull request this loop opened is recorded in `steps.json` and its body is updated once per run; a pull request the loop did not open gets a comment only. Reopens: never.
21. **Second opinion: sentinels, 60-id batches, one launch per batch.** Sentinels prove the agent read each input and feed the low-tier gate credit. Batches of 60 keep one answer within a size the orchestrator can check position by position; the number is revisited when a run shows a reason. There is no follow-up call and no inline input form: an unacknowledged input fails the stage, and the ladder relaunches. Reopens: a run where batching changes the verdict.
22. **Four audit commands.** `run`, `resume`, `act` and `handoff` are thin forwarders; `resume`, `act` and `handoff` take their few flags because they take ids and files, not prose. The prose form "reimagine audit ..." is the primary entry. Reopens: never.
23. **`commit: true` snapshots go to `docs/runs/<branch-slug>/`.** Each publishing run commits the plan and `report-<n>.md` with state `publishing`; the terminal report is posted as a pull request comment. Reopens: a repository convention that names another place, which `repo-docs` placement would then follow.
24. **Mechanisms not built, and why.** A one-request-per-session implementer transport: slices run in parallel when their files are disjoint. A model-provider availability probe before a run: the host is the only provider. Request files and thread ids for reviewers: each review is a fresh Agent call with the request in its prompt. A no-retry rule for a process that may outlive its call: subagents end with their call. A 3,600-second cap on implementer timeouts: budgets are minutes in `.reimagine.json`. A headless cap on the second opinion: it is an Agent call under the orchestrator's budget. Running the second opinion from the repository root: the agent reads absolute paths. A version gate between plugins: no plugin depends on another. An on-off option for a provider: there is one provider. Sending an empty diff to review: the step records "no changes" instead. Each is recorded so nobody adds it without a new reason. Reopens: never.
25. **The staleness notice display is an acceptance item.** Spike 3 showed the hook running with outcome success and the `systemMessage` JSON on stdout, but a headless run cannot show what the user sees. Reopens: never; it is checked interactively.
26. **The idempotency spike ran on git primitives only.** Spike 7 verified merge-base, explicit-refspec fetch, fast-forward over a foreign commit, an append-only record under `.reimagine/` excluded from status, the remote-head check, the three-dot diff, and the existence of `gh pr edit --body-file` and `gh pr comment --body-file`. A second invocation of the skill on a branch with an open pull request cannot run before the skill exists, and opening a pull request needs its own ask. That run is acceptance item 10. Reopens: never.
27. **CI pins a Claude Code version at least seven days old.** The validate job installs a fixed version with the autoupdater disabled so a new release cannot change the result without a commit here. 2.1.296 is the version the spikes ran on, and the workflow pins it. Reopens: a validation change in a newer host release.
29. **One script for the rules plugin.** The staleness notice is the `session-start` verb of `rules.mjs` rather than a module of its own; the script budget is 700 lines. Reopens: never.
30. **No loop commands at all.** "plan only" is a mode read from prose, and the skill itself is named `run`, so `/loop:run` is the skill and there is no command file. Reopens: never.
31. **Deferred audit helpers.** A stage 4 and 5 bookkeeping helper, `ledger.sh build6`, `report-parts`, `count`, and an Azure DevOps exporter are not built; each needs fixtures and a design review of its own. Reopens: a field run where hand bookkeeping slipped, recorded as an issue.
32. **The marketplace `metadata.version` is the catalog's own.** With no suite version, it is raised whenever an entry changes, and lint only requires it to be present and well formed. Reopens: never.
33. **Implementers are told to stay in scope.** A live run saw an implementer widen a `gh` command unasked and a brief cause a defect; requirement 104 makes the brief say so, and the slice review catches it. Reopens: never.
34. **Baseline failures match by test name.** Two of sixteen baseline failures in a live run passed later, so counts are not a stable match. Reopens: never.
35. **The loop plugin keeps its name; its skill is `run`.** Claude Code 2.1.296 bundles a `/loop` skill that runs a prompt on an interval, so the bare `/loop` is taken. The plugin stays `loop` (decision 2), its single skill is named `run` and answers only as `/loop:run`, "reimagine loop" is the primary trigger phrase and "run the loop" the secondary one, and acceptance item 25 checks that both reach this skill and that the bare `/loop` is the host's. Renaming the plugin was rejected because it would change every document and the catalog before anything is built. Settled 2026-10-14 by the owner. Reopens: acceptance item 25 showing the secondary phrase reaching the bundled skill, which would drop the phrase.
36. **An existing record is found by ticket id.** The predicted branch is built from every ticket in request order, so adding or reordering tickets changed the name, missed the record and would have opened a second pull request. Step 0.2 reads the `tickets` list of each record's `steps.json` before it predicts a name; one match is continued, several stop the run with the "continue <branch>" form. Requirements 49 and 83 name this lookup. Settled 2026-10-15. Reopens: a field run where the stop for several matches costs more than naming the branch.
37. **Steps 1 and 2 record input versions, not the hash of `inputs.md`.** Every run appends a section to `inputs.md`, so its hash never matches on a rerun and an interrupted run could not resume after Step 2. Each open input records the version its host reports, or the hash of its file. A comment on an open ticket counts as a change and reruns its verification, which is the safe side. Settled 2026-10-15; acceptance item 10(e) resumed at Step 3 with it. Reopens: a host whose version moves without an edit often enough to cost reruns.
38. **Per-run files take a run suffix.** Requirement 86 forbids rewriting a prior run's files, and the body, ticket comment and patch names were fixed, so from run 2 on they take `-run<n>`; the counters keep `pre-<n>`, `comment-<n>` and `text-<k>` apart. Settled 2026-10-15. Reopens: never.
39. **A ticket done at preflight gets no comment.** Requirement 90 says a run with no new work posts nothing, and a run that adds tickets reports on the new ones; a status comment on a finished ticket in every run would only repeat itself. Settled 2026-10-15. Reopens: a reviewer asking for a status line on every ticket of the pull request.
40. **The posted report leaves out the header lines that name local paths.** Under `commit: true` the record, worktree, repositories, handoff and audit lines name paths on the user's machine, which no published text names, so the external form drops them and keeps the rest of the header. Under `commit: true` that comment is the run's one comment on the pull request. Settled 2026-10-15. Reopens: never.
41. **No new work ends `done` only on published work.** A run whose inputs are all done counts its work as published when the run that last wrote Step 7 ended `done` and its pull request is open; otherwise Step 7 runs, pushes what the remote lacks and watches CI. Without this rule a run whose commits were never pushed, or whose CI was red, would end `done` with nothing anyone can review. Requirement 90 states the rule. Settled 2026-10-15 after a code review of the idempotency rules. Reopens: a host where the loop cannot tell whether its work is published.
42. **A slice committed by hand is found by its file hashes.** A `prepared` run leaves its work uncommitted and the user commits it with the report's commands, so no commit line in `run.md` names it. Each slice a run built and did not commit records the blob hash of each file it owns, and a later run counts it committed when the branch tip holds those blobs. Matching a foreign commit by its paths alone was rejected, since an unrelated commit can touch the same files. Settled 2026-10-15. Reopens: field runs where users edit the work before committing it, which leaves the slice open.
43. **A record whose pull request merged or closed is finished.** A reopened ticket finds its record by ticket id, and without this rule the run would judge it done or continue a branch whose pull request is closed. Preflight ends `blocked`, naming the pull request and the "on branch <name>" form that starts a new branch. Starting a new branch on its own was rejected because it changes branch prediction (requirements 77 and 83). Settled 2026-10-15 by the owner. Reopens: a team that reopens tickets often enough that the stop costs more than a suffixed branch name.
44. **No `inline_cap_bytes` fault.** The second-opinion request has one form: it names every input copy by path and carries no inline content, so no size cap exists for a fault to lower. Requirement 187 lists the six remaining `_test` keys. Settled 2026-10-15. Reopens: an inline request form.
45. **Two disclosed writes under the git directory.** An approved fetch writes remote-tracking refs, and `working-tree.sh build` writes loose objects for a `head: working-tree` bundle; both are listed in the read-only boundary of `common.md` and in the report. Requirement 175 names them as the only exceptions to "never writes under the git directory", since the two sentences read as a contradiction without it. Settled 2026-10-15. Reopens: a way to pin a working tree without an object write.
47. **Prerelease versions `0.1.0-alpha.<n>`.** A built plugin ships at a semver prerelease that sorts below 0.1.0, so the first release stays 0.1.0 and an installed alpha updates to it. The audit's `plugin_version` literal follows the manifest, so a run made under an alpha reruns stage 1 under the next version. Settled 2026-10-15. Reopens: never.
48. **Prerelease tags get changelog headings.** A tag `<plugin>--v0.1.0-alpha.<n>` is a tagged version like any other, so requirement 15 gives it a heading; a release with no tag gets none. Settled 2026-10-15. Reopens: never.
49. **A hub is a file with a `## Spokes` line.** The drift hook runs in every repository once the plugin is installed, so a long `CLAUDE.md` in a repository that never ran `/repo-docs` would have every commit denied for its length. The hook treats the root `CLAUDE.md`, or the `AGENTS.md` that an exact `@AGENTS.md` adapter names, as a hub only when it holds a line exactly `## Spokes`; otherwise it prints nothing, which is how requirement 210's "no hub" is checked. The adapter is one line, `@AGENTS.md`, with an LF or CRLF ending or none; an adapter whose `AGENTS.md` is missing is no hub. Settled 2026-10-15. Reopens: a request to warn in repositories not yet adopted.
50. **The hook checks the repository the commit runs in.** The base directory is the `cwd` field of the hook's input, else the hook's own working directory; each `-C <dir>` and `--work-tree <dir>` of the commit is applied to it as git applies `-C`, and `git rev-parse --show-toplevel` gives the root. A leading `~` in a directory becomes `$HOME`; a directory that does not resolve, such as one written with a shell variable the hook never expands, falls back to the base directory. A `cd` before the commit in the same command is not followed. Git itself runs only for that root and for the changed set of decision 56; requirement 211's list names the text tools the hook may use besides it. Settled 2026-10-15. Reopens: a field commit after a `cd` into another repository that the hook missed.
52. **Spoke indexes and coverage.** A spoke's own pointers are the column-0 `- ` lines under its own `## Spokes` line, in the same form, followed to any depth with each file read once; prose bullets elsewhere in a spoke are never read as pointers. An index runs from `## Spokes` to the next level 1 or 2 heading. A pointer's coverage is every backticked token without a space in its condition clause, the text after the first full stop and space outside backticks, that names an existing file or directory, so a word in the first sentence or a bare command name never becomes a prefix; a pointer with none covers its spoke's directory when the spoke sits beside code, and nothing when it sits at the root or under `docs/`, since a `docs/` page would otherwise cover every other page there. Settled 2026-10-15. Reopens: a spoke layout this misses, seen in the field.
53. **What pins a file, and what a move commit holds.** Requirement 204 forbids moving a file something depends on, naming links, while requirement 205 repairs inbound links in the same change as a move. A file is pinned by an `@` import of it, by a reference from a file that is not documentation (code, configuration, CI, a package manifest), or by a fixed name other tools read (`README.md`, `CLAUDE.md`, `AGENTS.md`, `CONTRIBUTING.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `LICENSE`, anything under `.github/`). A link from a documentation file does not pin it: the move commit holds the `git mv` and the path edits of those links and nothing else, so no commit leaves a broken link and every rewrite goes in a later commit. Requirement 13 governs this repository's commits, not the user's. Settled 2026-10-15. Reopens: an amendment of requirement 204 that says this itself.
54. **Who commits.** Bootstrap writes the hub and leaves it uncommitted, since it asks nothing. Adopt, and maintain when it finds drift, print a plan that lists every commit, ask once, and make those commits, so one yes answers the user's own ask-before-commit rule; maintain with nothing to fix writes nothing. A second run after adopt finds the hub and reads as maintain, which is how adopt is idempotent. Each commit body opens with `Instruction-file <mode> pass, <date>.`, so the mode is on record when the printed mode line is not seen. Settled 2026-10-15. Reopens: a request for bootstrap to commit.
55. **A nested `AGENTS.md` without a root one stays.** Requirement 202 says what a repository that keeps `AGENTS.md` does, but not what a lone nested `AGENTS.md` beside a `CLAUDE.md` hub means. A repository keeps `AGENTS.md` when the root one exists; a nested one without it is read by other tools by that name, so adopt leaves it in place and the hub points to it as a directory spoke. An import of an untracked file is reported and left in place, neither inlined nor made a pointer, since a pointer to it fails on every other clone. Settled 2026-10-15. Reopens: a team that wants the nested file converted.
56. **The recheck advice reads what the commit records, not only the index.** The hook runs before the command, so the index alone misses files the same command stages: `git add x && git commit`, `git commit -a` and a pathspec commit, the first being how Claude usually commits. The changed set is `git diff --cached --name-only`, `git diff HEAD --name-only`, and, when the command runs `git add`, `git ls-files --others --exclude-standard`. This can name a spoke for a tracked change the commit leaves out, which costs one needless recheck; missing the usual commit form cost every recheck. Requirement 209 names the index alone; the proposed amendment names this set. Settled 2026-10-15 after a code review of the milestone. Reopens: field reports of advice for changes the commit did not hold.
57. **Pointer paths and caps are checked in the working tree.** The hook checks that each named path exists and counts lines in the files on disk, not in what the commit records, since a commit's content cannot be known before the command runs (`-a`, pathspecs, `git add` in the same command). So a pointer to an ignored file passes on the machine that has it, and an unstaged edit to the hub decides a commit of unrelated files. The skill never points to an untracked file (decision 55). Settled 2026-10-15 after a code review of the milestone. Reopens: a pointer to an ignored file reaching a shared branch.
58. **Versions stay at `0.1.0-alpha.1` until the release milestone.** `main` is a release channel: an installed plugin stays cached until its version string changes, so a version change is what reaches a machine that installed from the catalog. Setting 0.1.0 in the catalog milestone would publish an untagged 0.1.0 before lint, CI and the release script exist. The catalog milestone adds only `metadata.version` (decision 32), at `0.1.0-alpha.1`; the release milestone sets every plugin and the catalog to 0.1.0 with `tools/release.mjs`, which is that script's first real use. Settled 2026-10-16. Reopens: the owner wanting 0.1.0 on a second machine before the release milestone.
59. **The drift hook reaches sessions on this repository through project settings.** Nothing in the suite edits a user's `settings.json`, so requirement 16 uses the host's project scope: a tracked `.claude/settings.json` declares this catalog under `extraKnownMarketplaces` with its GitHub source, the one users install from, and sets `repo-docs@reimagine-engine` in `enabledPlugins`. On a scratch profile, a person who opened a clone saw no dialog beyond the usual folder trust, and the host cloned the catalog and cached `repo-docs`; a headless session in that clone still loaded no plugin and let a commit through. After `claude plugin install repo-docs@reimagine-engine --scope project`, which wrote the same settings and recorded the install in the profile, a headless session listed `repo-docs` in its start event and the hook denied a commit whose hub named a missing path. So the README asks for that install once per machine. A `directory` source pointing at the checkout was rejected: it carries the same catalog name as the GitHub one a user installs, and its relative path from project settings is not documented. Settled 2026-10-16. Reopens: a host release that loads a project-enabled plugin headless without an install. Since decision 181 one script of the suite does edit the user settings file, `guard.mjs`, within requirement 336; nothing else does (requirement 43).
61. **Lint's rules live in one Node script.** `tests/audit/lint.sh` checks the audit plugin's agent tools, `gh` hosts and merger shape in awk as part of that plugin's suite; `tools/lint.mjs` implements the repository-wide forms of R219 in Node without calling it, so `npm run lint` needs no `sh` and one file holds every rule CI enforces. The audit suite keeps its own checks. Settled 2026-10-16. Reopens: the two drifting apart on a rule both hold.
62. **What the tag rule reads.** Lint reads tags with `git tag --list`, a plugin's last tag being its highest by semver, and a plugin directory changed since that tag when `git diff --quiet <tag> -- plugins/<name>/` exits 1 or the directory holds an untracked file that is not ignored. Outside a git work tree, or in a shallow clone, the tag and changelog checks fail with a message saying to fetch the full history, rather than pass on missing data. A clone with history but no tags is caught by the changelog: a heading whose version is not the plugin's current one must have its tag, so a missing tag fails with "fetch the tags". CI checks out with `fetch-depth: 0`, which brings the tags. Settled 2026-10-16. Reopens: a release flow that writes a heading for a version it never tags.
63. **What "owner-specific" matches without a list.** Lint holds no names (decision 10), so R10's rule matches forms: an email address whose domain does not end in `.invalid` (the `git@` of an SSH remote excepted); a path under `/Users/`, `/home/` or a drive's `Users` folder whose user part is not a placeholder (`<...>`, `{...}`, a variable, `~`, or one of the words user, username, you, me, dev, name, example, runner); and a GitHub, GitLab, Bitbucket or Azure DevOps URL whose owner is not a placeholder (owner, org, example, acme, octo-org, octocat, `example-` names, `<...>`, `{...}`, `:owner`) and that is not this repository. A person's name cannot be told from other words, so it is left to the deny tokens and review; so is R44's rule that the chat block names no vendor, since a vendor list in lint would hold names decision 10 keeps out of the repository. Settled 2026-10-16. Reopens: an owner-specific string in a shipped file that these forms miss.
64. **CI pins Claude Code 2.1.296.** Decision 27 asks for a version at least seven days old by its npm publish date; `npm view @anthropic-ai/claude-code time` gives 2.1.296 as 2026-10-09T16:58:10Z, so it qualifies from 2026-10-16T16:58Z, and it is the version the spikes and the first validation runs used. The validate job installs it with `npm install -g` under `DISABLE_AUTOUPDATER=1` and runs `claude plugin validate --strict` on the root and each plugin directory with `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` in the runner's temporary directory; validation needs no login, shown by a run under an empty configuration directory. The mawk job puts a link named `awk` to `mawk` first on `PATH`, since the audit scripts call `awk` and the ubuntu image may resolve it to another awk, and checks the version line before the suites run. Actions are pinned by commit sha, kept current by Dependabot. `workflow_dispatch` lets a branch run the workflow before it reaches `main`. Settled 2026-10-16. Reopens: a validation change in a newer host release, or a pinned version that fails to install.
65. **The first Code item asks for a change that stays correct.** In "Make the smallest correct change", "smallest" outweighed "correct": a minimal change patched the symptom, left a sibling site unchanged, or bypassed an existing helper, and review found the defect. The rewritten item, requirement 232, anchors correctness on the code as it grows, asks to follow the code's patterns and change every place the behavior lives, and lets a known next change shape the design without building it. The one guard against over-engineering it keeps is structure for an imagined need. A rule on the count or size of tests was considered and rejected for lack of evidence that tests were too many; the Tests and Done items already ask for a test per behavior change. Adds to decision 4. Settled 2026-10-18. Reopens: review findings of the same kinds under the new wording, or changes that build for a need no one named.
66. **A decision names its frame, and a wrong request is a decision.** Options that share an assumption hide it, since a reader comparing them sees only what differs. The decision item, requirement 233, names the assumption every option shares and whether it was checked, and what would overturn the recommendation, so a reviewer can judge the frame as well as the choice. A request that is itself wrong is raised as the first decision, with the evidence and what to do instead, requirement 234, rather than carried out in silence. Adds to decision 4. Settled 2026-10-18. Reopens: replies where the added parts are filler, or a raised decision that holds up work the request plainly wanted.
67. **A denied call is a question, never routed around.** A call the permission mode or a hook denies is reported at once and in full, and the work waits, requirement 236; another command, a script or a subagent that reaches the same effect defeats the denial. Naming the steps likely to be denied before starting, requirement 235, lets the owner allow them up front. Advice on when to switch permission modes was considered and left out: it describes one way of working, not a rule for every user. Adds to decision 4. Settled 2026-10-18. Reopens: a host release that turns a denied call into a permission prompt by itself.
68. **The chat block takes the style's Links rule.** A session in the chat app can have a repository, a shell and tool results, so it can run `git remote -v` and read a URL a tool returned. The block now carries the style's Links lead sentence and its Azure DevOps remote-to-base sentence, and the difference that left them out is gone. What still differs is what the app's context calls for: commit messages follow the repo's convention, and three additions, the rule for editing the user's text, marking what a doc or a long answer assumed, and one recommendation with what would overturn it, in step with the Working decision rule. The block is the style plus those additions; rules for app sessions would be a second block, not a longer one. No size is enforced: the app publishes no cap for its instructions field, and the 3,995-character block saved there whole on 2026-10-18, so the header asks only that the block stay short and lint checks only that there is one block. Settled 2026-10-18. Reopens: evidence from app sessions that the house rules are missed, or a field that cuts the block off.
69. **The higher-risk review keeps its range.** A claim that `<merge-base>...HEAD` leaves out the uncommitted slice work was not reproduced. On 2026-10-19 the review skill, given that range over a tree with one commit past the base and one uncommitted intent-to-add file, ran `git diff <base>...HEAD` and then `git diff HEAD`, and covered both files; SKILL.md marks new files with `git add -N` before every pass. On 2026-10-22, acceptance item 40 under auto mode confirmed it: both rounds got an empty range on a new branch and reviewed the working-tree diff. Requirement 63 is unchanged. Settled 2026-10-19. Reopens: a pass seen to leave out uncommitted work, since on a new branch the range is empty at Step 5 and the review rests on the skill reading the working tree.
70. **A merger reads in pages, and the split thresholds stay.** On 2026-10-19 the host's Read refused a 341 KB file, and capped a read at 25,000 tokens: a 199,392-byte, 1,500-line file read whole came back as lines 1 to 867 with a partial-view notice, a 2,000-line page was refused, and 600-line pages read a 349,492-byte file to its end. A byte threshold cannot hold under a token cap whose bytes per token vary with the text, so each merger reads every file it takes whole in pages of at most 600 lines, halved on a refusal, checks it reached the line count its prompt gives, and fails with `status: failed at <file>:<line>` when it cannot. The 450,000-byte thresholds size the merger's context, not one read, and stay. The merger gains Grep, a search tool and not a shell, to find a ledger section it opens for a duplicate check. Settled 2026-10-19. Reopens: a host Read that changes its caps, or a merger that runs out of context under the threshold.
71. **The agents' command lists name the read-only git commands they reach for.** Agents ran `git rev-parse`, `git merge-base`, `git branch --list`, `git ls-tree`, `cut` and `uniq` outside the list, each read-only, logged and passed by the read-only check, in this suite's acceptance runs and in use. All five copies of the list now hold them and `git cat-file` with `-t`, `-s` or `-p`; `sort -o`, `--output` and `--compress-program` and `uniq` with an output file operand are barred, since those forms write or run a program. The skill pre-approves the same reads for the orchestrator. A rule that makes a logged read outside the list a note was left out: it needed its own procedure, and the additions cover the commands seen. Settled 2026-10-19. Reopens: a run where agents reach for another command outside the list.
72. **A non-GitHub host gets the body its prepared report names, and Azure DevOps gets its own ticket form.** A run on a host other than GitHub ended `prepared` after Step 6 without reading `pr-body.md`, yet its report named a body file. The end of Step 6 now writes it. Microsoft's documentation says `AB#<n>` links a work item only from GitHub through the Azure Boards app, and its REST API caps a pull request description at 4,000 characters, counted in UTF-16 units. So on an Azure DevOps remote a ticket is a link to `_workitems/edit/<n>` named by its type, and the body is always a short form, the related pull requests, the closing lines and a pointer, with the full body in a comment file to post first. The length is not measured: `wc` is not pre-approved, and a denied call in Steps 0 to 6 ends the run `blocked`. A `#<n>` mention was not used, because a pasted `#<n>` linking is unverified, while a link renders anywhere. A `Refs` line does not attach the work item to the pull request, so the report asks the user to link it with the host's tooling. Settled 2026-10-19. Reopens: a verified `#<n>` attach in a pasted description, a run whose closing lines alone pass the cap (about 35 tickets), or a tested `az repos pr create` path (decision 7).
73. **The `primer` plugin replaces `repo-docs`.** The host now loads path-scoped rules, nested `CLAUDE.md` files and skills on its own and writes auto memory, so the spoke index that told a model when to read a file has no job left; what the host still lacks is the placement judgment, a short hub, adopt for a repository not built this way, verification against the code and a commit-time check, and those carry over. The name is `primer`, a plain noun for what primes a session that collides with no bundled command, so `/primer` is the skill; `context` was rejected because it reads as the host's `/context`. It is a new plugin, not a rename: `repo-docs` retires at 0.1.1 with its catalog entry removed, its tags left and one README line saying adopt converts a repo-docs repository. The spec's two additions, proposing project skills and promoting auto memory, are the reason for the rework. Requirements 237 to 268; requirements 197 to 212 retired. Settled 2026-10-20 by the owner. Reopens: never.
74. **The hub marker is `<!-- primer -->` on line 1.** Spike 1: an `InstructionsLoaded` hook fired for a root `CLAUDE.md` whose first line was that comment, and the session's context, read through a prompt that quoted its instructions, held no trace of the comment; the same held for a root `AGENTS.md` read directly, for which no `InstructionsLoaded` event fired at all. The marker costs no context and tells the hook the repository opted in; `## Spokes` with an empty index spent two lines of every session. Settled 2026-10-20 by the owner. Reopens: a host release that stops stripping HTML comments, which the hook test on a fixture cannot see and acceptance item 27 would.
75. **A root `AGENTS.md` is the hub and no `CLAUDE.md` is written.** The host documentation read 2026-10-20 says a root `AGENTS.md` is read in place of `CLAUDE.md` from 2.1.277 when no `CLAUDE.md` or `CLAUDE.local.md` exists in the working directory or above it, and that a `CLAUDE.md` holding `@AGENTS.md` still works and is never read twice. So the plugin writes no adapter, says once that an older session needs the one-line `CLAUDE.md`, keeps an adapter it finds, and proposes the adapter when a root `CLAUDE.local.md` exists, since that file stops the direct read. Rules and skills are Claude-only files, so a multi-tool repository keeps shared facts in `AGENTS.md` and Claude's own under `.claude/`. The all-or-nothing adapter convention of requirement 202 goes. Settled 2026-10-20 by the owner. Reopens: a field repository where sessions below 2.1.277 are common, which would make the adapter the default again.
76. **The memory notice ships, and where it reads.** It is the shape of the rules notice: SessionStart at `startup`, one `systemMessage` line, nothing written. Spike 3 showed that a `MEMORY.md` line carries a title, a file name and a hook and no type or date, so the hook reads each topic file the index lists for its `type` and `modified`, not one file as the spec assumed. The stamp lives at `${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_DATA}/reviewed/<key>` and is written by the skill after a promote pass; a headless session refuses writes under the configuration directory under `acceptEdits` and makes them under `auto`, as the promote milestone's acceptance run of 2026-10-21 showed, so a headless acceptance run under `acceptEdits` sees the count unchanged. The notice stays quiet in a repository whose hub lacks the marker, the same opt-in rule as the drift hook, so an installed plugin never speaks in a repository that did not adopt it. Settled 2026-10-20 by the owner. Reopens: a host release that adds a type or date to the index line, which would let the hook read one file.
77. **Maintain's verify pass is the plugin's own; `/doctor prompt-audit` is named, not called.** Spike 4: the Skill tool refused `doctor` with "cannot be used with Skill tool due to disable-model-invocation" and the host's note not to replicate its workflow by other means; it accepted `claude-api prompt-audit`, which is the bundled skill `/doctor prompt-audit` runs through, and `init`. So a plugin can reach the audit, but through a name the host documents as an implementation detail, and the spec's non-goals forbid running `/init`, `/doctor` or `/memory`. The plugin checks commands, paths and statements against the code itself, as repo-docs did, and the report names `/doctor prompt-audit` for the contradiction and stale-model pass. Settled 2026-10-20 by the owner. Reopens: the owner asking for the audit to run inside maintain, which the spike shows the `claude-api prompt-audit` route would allow.
78. **Caps 60, 200 and 150.** Hub and nested `CLAUDE.md` 60 lines, rule 200, `SKILL.md` 150; the host's own numbers, 200 per instruction file and 500 per skill, are ceilings with a warning, not targets. The counts are the lines the file has, frontmatter and blank lines included, since that is what the host warns on. Settled 2026-10-20 by the owner. Reopens: a host change in context handling.
79. **A promoted note is offered for deletion per note, as a separate question.** The memory directory is the user's, the host says a note that `CLAUDE.md` already holds is skipped, and an index that keeps promoted notes grows past the 200 lines the host loads. The deletion question comes after the project write and a no leaves the note. Settled 2026-10-20 by the owner. Reopens: never.
80. **A `paths` glob with no `/` is matched at every depth; the hook prefixes `**/`.** Spike 2 on fourteen rules: the host and `git ls-files -- ':(glob)<pattern>'` listed the same files for `**/*.ts` (depth zero included), `src/**/*`, `src/components/*.tsx`, `src/[ab].ts`, `docs/*.md`, the comma string `lib/*.ts, tests/**/*.test.ts`, and the brace forms `src/**/*.{ts,tsx}` and `{src,lib}/**/*.ts` once the hook expanded the braces, since git's pathspec has none; an invalid `photos [2024/**` matched nothing on both. They differed on `*.md`, `*.tsx` and `[ab].ts`: the host loaded the rule for a file at any depth, where its documentation says root only, and git listed root files only. The host records `src/**` as `src`, and git lists everything under `src` for both. So the hook prefixes `**/` to a pattern with no slash. Settled 2026-10-20. Reopens: a host release that matches a slashless pattern at the root only, which the hook test cannot see and acceptance item 27 would catch.
81. **A `paths` value the host cannot read never loads the rule; the hook denies.** Spike 2: a rule with `paths: [unclosed` never loaded in two sessions, at launch or on a matching touch, where the documentation says unparseable frontmatter makes the rule unscoped; `paths: "src/**` with the quote unterminated did not load either; a valid list beside a bad key loaded scoped. Either way the author's intent is lost without a word from the host, so the finding reads "has a paths value the host cannot read". Settled 2026-10-20. Reopens: a host release that reports the parse failure itself.
82. **A rule or skill names a `docs/` file in words; imports live in `CLAUDE.md` files only.** Spike 5: an `@docs/style.md` line inside `.claude/rules/` produced no `InstructionsLoaded` event for the imported file at launch or when the rule loaded for a matching file, and the model quoted the line as literal text. The spec assumed the host expands it. So requirement 243 has a rule or skill name the file with when to read it, the hook checks no imports in rules, and the only import the hub may hold is `@AGENTS.md`. Settled 2026-10-20. Reopens: a host release that expands imports in rules, confirmed by rerunning the spike.
83. **Where auto memory lives, and how the notice finds it.** Spike 3 on three sessions with corrections: the memory directory is `<config>/projects/<key>/memory/`, where `<key>` is the absolute path of the main worktree's root with every character outside `A-Za-z0-9` replaced by `-` (`s3_u.v` became `s3-u-v`); a session in a linked worktree used the main checkout's memory directory while its transcript sat under its own working directory's key, so the directory of the transcript is the wrong base. The hook takes `<config>` from the grandparent of its input's `transcript_path` and `<key>` from the parent of `git rev-parse --git-common-dir`, falling back to the working directory outside a repository. A topic file holds frontmatter `name`, `description` and `metadata` with `node_type`, `type`, `originSessionId` and `modified` in ISO 8601; the index line is `- [Title](file.md) ` followed by an em dash and the hook text, with no type or date. The test fixture writes that index with the em dash as an octal escape, so the fixture stays ASCII. Decision 214 adds the two settings that move the directory. Settled 2026-10-20. Reopens: a layout change, which the fixture test would show as a failure of the notice.
84. **A skill with `paths` is hidden until a matching file is touched; phrases in the description invoke it.** Spike 6: a skill whose description named its phrases was invoked in 2 of 2 runs where the prompt said one, and not in the control; a skill with `paths` was absent from the listing until the session read a matching file, when the host activated it by name and the model invoked it through the Skill tool, and a session that touched only other files never saw it. So the session source of requirement 253 rests on the description's phrases, and a `paths` skill never fires for an unrelated task. Settled 2026-10-20. Reopens: a trigger miss in acceptance. Decision 318 takes the phrases out of primer's description; the session source is now handed to `/primer` by the user.
85. **The hook's deny and advice reach the model under auto mode.** Spike 7 with the repo-docs hook as the stand-in: under `--permission-mode auto` the advice arrived as `PreToolUse:PowerShell hook additional context: ...` and the model repeated it, and a deny arrived as `PreToolUse:Bash hook error: ...` with the commit absent from the log. The primer hook keeps both forms. Settled 2026-10-20. Reopens: a host release that drops `additionalContext` from a denied or allowed call.
86. **Coverage is the host's own declaration.** The advice of requirement 263 reads a rule's or skill's `paths` and the directory of a nested `CLAUDE.md`, over the changed set of decision 56; the pointer grammar, the backticked prefixes and the `## Spokes` index of decision 52 go, since each existed to make a model read a file the host now loads itself. A hub path check stays for the backticked tokens of the opening paragraph that look like a path, holding a `/` or ending in `.md` with no space, `<`, `>`, `*`, `{` or `?`, so a placeholder such as `plugins/<name>/` or a command name is never checked, and a token that starts with `~`, `/` or a drive letter, a URL, and a token that does not end in `.md` and whose first segment is no top-level entry of the repository, such as the branch `origin/main`, are not paths in the repository and are not checked, since each of those denied every commit of a repository whose hub named it; that sentence is the one pointer form that remains, and this repository's hub passes it once adopt puts the design document paths in backticks. Settled 2026-10-20. Reopens: a hub token the check passes that names a path which was moved or deleted.
89. **CI keeps 2.1.296 while the primer spikes record 2.1.304.** The spikes ran on the version then installed; decision 27 asks the pin to be at least seven days old by its publish date, and a pin moves only for a validation change, which no spike showed. Acceptance records for primer name the version they ran on. Settled 2026-10-20. Reopens: a validation change in a newer host release, as decision 64 says.
90. **The drift hook's open details.** Requirements 261 to 264 leave these to the hook. `.claude/CLAUDE.md` is not a nested file: the host loads it at launch with the hub and adopt merges it into the hub (requirement 244), so it has no cap check and covers no directory. A skill's `paths` is read and checked as a rule's is, with the same two findings, since a skill whose glob matches nothing never activates (decision 84). A `paths` value is unreadable when it opens a flow list with `[` and does not close it, opens a quote it does not close, or is empty with no list item under it; spike 2 showed the host lenient beyond that, loading a valid list scoped beside a key YAML cannot parse, so no other YAML rule is enforced. A comma splits a flow list or a comma string only outside quotes and brace groups, so `"src/**/*.{ts,tsx}"` stays one glob. Braces are expanded first and `**/` is prefixed to each expanded pattern with no `/`; a finding names the glob as written. When the command runs `git add`, untracked files that are not ignored count for a glob as they count as changed under decision 56, so `git add -A && git commit` of a new rule with the files it covers is not denied. The opening paragraph's backticked tokens pair across its lines, and each distinct token is checked once, in order. A file the commit removes from the index is in no glob's file list, so its removal names nothing to recheck. Settled 2026-10-20. Reopens: a host release that loads `.claude/CLAUDE.md` per directory or reads `paths` strictly, or a field deny on a glob the host matched.
91. **Primer never edits `CONTRIBUTING.md`; a procedure there becomes a skill and stays.** Acceptance items 29 and 33 said the procedure leaves `CONTRIBUTING.md`, while requirement 246 leaves that file out of what primer writes, requirement 253 lists it under the repository rather than the instruction files, and requirement 255 changes nothing else for a skill taken from the repository. The file is for people, as `README.md` is, and decision 53 already pins it by name. So adopt and propose raise its procedure as a skill candidate with the file as its source and leave the file unchanged, and the two items say so. Settled 2026-10-21 by the owner. Reopens: the owner asking for agent procedures to leave contributor documents, which would add `CONTRIBUTING.md` to requirement 246 and treat it as an instruction file in adopt.
92. **The review stamp is one line, the UTC time of the review in the form of a note's `modified`.** Requirement 258 compares each note's `modified` with the stamp `${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_DATA}/reviewed/<key>` and leaves the stamp's content open. Spike 3 showed the host writing `modified` as `2026-10-20T16:16:34.473Z`, so the skill writes the stamp as `date -u +%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%S.000Z` prints it and the notice compares the two as text, which awk does on every platform with no date parsing; a file's modification time would need `stat`, whose flags differ between GNU and BSD. A note modified within the second of the review counts as unreviewed once more, which costs one extra notice at most. The memory fixture ships its stamp beside `memory/`, since its `<key>` is known only where it is placed. Settled 2026-10-21. Reopens: a host release that writes `modified` in another form, which acceptance item 34 would show.
93. **Finding issues is judgment, not a count.** Asked to find issues, review, log feedback or propose changes, the model treated the list as the deliverable and found as many items as it could; the owner restated the bar by hand in every session, and the backlog that resulted is where the debt that forced the rewrite of the previous suite came from. The house rules carried the fixing-side rule (requirement 232: a need only imagined gets no option, abstraction or fallback) and the scope rule ("Stay inside the request") and nothing for the finding side. The item is true of every project, which is where placement item 11 of requirement 241 puts a fact: the house rules. The host's documentation names the behavior once, in its best-practices page on adversarial review ("a reviewer prompted to find gaps will usually report some, even when the work is sound, because that is what it was asked to do"), and offers a per-prompt sentence as the fix; nothing in the host carries it as a default. The item, requirement 269, replaces a ranking with a test, the clause after "the few that": correctness (a bug or an unmet requirement), risk (harm that has not happened yet), or costs more to leave than to fix (debt that raises the next change's price, and gaps that cost users or operators time), so a long list cannot pass through sorted. "The request" is not a term, because "Stay inside the request" covers scope and an unmet requirement is a correctness finding; a feature idea with no measured cost of leaving it is a note by design; "evidence" inherits the Done items, so a claim counts only when the thing itself was checked and a cause is named only after the check that could rule it out. The Done item "Report anything else you notice, with evidence" is read with it: "anything else" lands as notes. The chat block gains the same rule in one line, which differs from the item in nothing, so the block's list of deliberate differences does not name it. Adds to decision 4. Settled 2026-10-22 by the owner. Reopens: review finding the two items in tension, which would make a ninth edit read "as a note, with evidence"; or the owner still restating the bar before `/primer` proposals or audit act lists after a few weeks of use, which would mean a per-plugin line, not a longer house rule.
94. **A dynamic workflow is an engine behind the audit's stages 4, 5 and 6, never a new stage model.** The stage inputs and outputs, the run directory, `stages.json`, the ledger scripts and the report keep their contracts; a stage records `engine: workflow` or `engine: prompt`, and the prompt path stays for a session where workflows are disabled, unavailable or refused at launch. Decision 21's sentinels and 60-id batches belong to the prompt path; the workflow path proves what each agent received from the script's own bookkeeping. The engine ships only when its run on the ground-truth fixture costs the same tokens or fewer than the prompt path on that fixture, both counts recorded; more tokens for the same findings is a reason not to build it, whatever else the spikes show. Settled 2026-10-21 by the owner. Reopens: field runs where the prompt path is never taken, which would retire it.
95. **A workflow script under `plugins/audit/workflows/` is an executable under requirement 6.** Its check is `node --check` and a lint rule on its `meta` block, plus the fixture acceptance item, because the runtime needs a session and no `node:test` suite can drive `agent()`. Settled 2026-10-21 by the owner. Reopens: a host harness that runs a workflow script without a session.
96. **`REVIEW.md` is read at the base commit of the bundle, never from the bundle's own changes**, as the host reads it from the base or default branch. Its severity calibration and skip rules shape the report; a finding a skip rule removes is counted under the rule that removed it, in one line, so nothing leaves the report silently. Settled 2026-10-21 by the owner. Reopens: never.
97. **Evals run locally before a release and never in CI; requirement 229 stands.** A case passes at 2 of 2 runs, the bar the phrase spikes used (decisions 14 and 84), and the score is recorded in acceptance with the host version. Settled 2026-10-21 by the owner. Reopens: a host eval runner that needs no model call, which would let CI run it.
98. **The suite's startup cost is measured at every release and recorded**; a lint cap on the sum of `description` bytes across every shipped skill, command and agent is set after two measurements, not before. Settled 2026-10-21 by the owner. Reopens: never.
99. **Every new acceptance result names the permission mode of its run**, and auto mode gets its own pass of the loop's full run, the ask-first publish item and the audit's low-tier run. Settled 2026-10-21 by the owner. Reopens: never.
100. **Eval cases live under `tests/evals/<plugin>/<case>/`, and the run targets the repository root.** Spike 5 on 2.1.304: a case file targeted by path may name a plugin only under its containment root, "the enclosing plugin for a target inside one you control, else the directory you ran `claude plugin eval` against", so `tests/evals/loop/prose-trigger/prompt.md` with `plugins: ["../../../../plugins/loop"]` failed to load when the case was the target and loaded when the target was the repository root with `--eval-dir tests/evals`; the root has no manifest and contributes nothing. Requirement 2 stays as it is. The loop case typed `reimagine loop #1, plan only` and primer's typed `check the instruction files`, each with `max_turns: 8` and `allowed_tools` of Read, Glob, Grep and Skill; each scored 2 of 2 with the plugin and 0 of 2 without, at a cost of about one dollar and three minutes for both. A `tool_used` grader on the Skill tool reported as not scored under the two-arm run, so it is a plugin-fired indicator, and the `regex` grader over the trace (`Tickets:`) or the last message (`^primer: mode (init|adopt|maintain)`) carries the assertion. Results land in `tests/evals/results/<timestamp>/`, which is ignored. A target of the form `<plugin>@<marketplace>` would read cases from the installed copy and was not tried. Settled 2026-10-22. Reopens: a host release that changes the containment root, which item 38 would show as a load failure.
101. **Workflow facts the engine rests on.** Spikes 1, 3 and 4 on 2.1.304, headless, with a throwaway plugin loaded by `--plugin-dir`: a plugin's `workflows/<name>.js` with a literal `meta` is listed as `/<plugin>:<name>` with and without the `workflows` manifest field, and `claude plugin validate --strict` passes both; `args` reaches the script as structured data; an `agent()` call with `schema` returns the object; `phase()` titles reach the progress events as `<phase>: <label>`; a run of two agents cost about 40,000 tokens, and the completion notification carries `total_tokens`, `tool_uses` and `duration_ms` but not which agents ran live. `import()` fails before launch with `Workflow script has a syntax error and was not launched: SyntaxError: import() is not available in workflow scripts.`; `Date.now()` and a no-argument `new Date()` fail at run time with `Date.now() / new Date() are unavailable in workflow scripts (breaks resume). Stamp results after the workflow returns, or pass timestamps via args.`, and `Math.random()` with `Math.random() is unavailable in workflow scripts (breaks resume). For N independent samples, include the index in the agent label or prompt.`; so the audit passes its timestamp in `args`. A script that threw after its first agent and was relaunched in the same session through `claude -p --resume <session>` with `scriptPath`, `resumeFromRunId` and changed `args` ran only its second agent, the journal showing the first served from its saved result, and returned the whole result; the journal keys each agent by a `v2:<hash>` value and the first agent, whose prompt did not read the changed argument, was served from it, so the key probably hashes the agent's prompt and options and `args` that change no prompt keep the cache; but the replay is the longest unchanged prefix of `agent()` calls in call order, as the host documents, which spike 3 on the spike 2 run showed: a `pipeline()` of nine auditors each followed by a refuter had its nine auditors replayed and all nine refuters rerun, since on the first run the refuters started in the auditors' completion order and on the relaunch in item order, so an engine that must resume calls its agents in a fixed order, a `parallel()` barrier per phase, and gives up the pipeline's overlap. A headless `claude -p` session whose turn ended while the workflow ran stopped the workflow with status `stopped` after about ten minutes, so a stage that launches one headless keeps its turn alive until the notification arrives. The audit's `stages.json` must hold the session id, the run id and the script path for its own resume to relaunch the same run. The Skill tool also lists a workflow's name, and under `claude -p` a session that may not use Skill reaches for it first and reports the bare denial text, so a stage that launches a workflow names the Workflow tool. Without a `Workflow` allow rule the launch is refused with `Review dynamic workflow before running` and the Skill route with `Execute skill: <plugin>:<name>`; with `Workflow(<plugin>:<name>)` it runs; with `disableWorkflows` the commands and the Workflow tool are absent and the Skill tool answers `Unknown skill: <plugin>:<name>`; under `--permission-mode auto` headless the first launch ran with no prompt, no entry in `permission_denials` and no change to the profile's `settings.json`, so the documented once-only consent is interactive, and the engine's acceptance item names the allow rule it ran under. Settled 2026-10-22. Reopens: a host release that changes any of these, which item 43 would show.
102. **Cost readings.** Spike 6 on 2.1.304, in the scratch profile with the four plugins installed from the catalog and the rules block applied: `claude -p "/context"` from an empty directory answered as a local command at no cost with the tables; the four plugins' skills summed to about 710 tokens (audit's five entries about 500, `loop:run` about 100, `primer:primer` about 110), the five audit agents to 430, and the profile's `CLAUDE.md` with the rules block to about 2,000, in a 19,600-token startup context of which the system tools took 9,300 and the host's own bundled skills about 2,300; plugin descriptions appear one line per skill, command and agent, not as one line per plugin. `claude plugin details` on each plugin directory gave the same shape: rules about 57 tokens always on, loop about 104, audit about 864, primer about 123, about 1,150 in all. The sum of `description` bytes across the thirteen shipped `SKILL.md`, command and agent files is 3,243, the number requirement 274's cap will be set against after the second reading. `claude -p "/skill-doctor"` answered that the command is not among the available commands, so its reading is interactive only and item 39 records it as not available when a session does not list it. Settled 2026-10-22. Reopens: never.
103. **The workflow engine is not built: the comparison shows the same tokens for the same findings, and resume needs a fixed call order.** The item 15 baseline ran on the prompt path on 2026-10-22: medium tier, 37 converged items, 18 of the 20 planted cases as items, 2,215 s whole, and for stages 4 to 6 nine auditors at 712,699 tokens, nine adversaries at 731,941 and one second opinion at 222,580, 1,667,220 in all, on the host's subagent token measure, which is each agent's final context size. Spike 2 ran the same nine scopes as a workflow on that run directory with the audit's own agent types: nine auditors at 731,739 tokens, one refuter per report at 769,374, a merger at 35,156, 1,536,269 in all; 54 pass-one findings against the prompt path's 56, 19 of the 20 planted cases by title in pass one and in the 42 merged items, one more than the prompt path, with the twentieth present only as the both-sides erratum in both runs. Like for like, pass one and pass two, the workflow cost 1,501,113 tokens against 1,444,640, four percent more, which is within what two runs of the prompt path differ by; its total is under the baseline only because it ran no second-opinion set, the agent the prompt path pays 222,580 for. Decision 94 builds the engine for a token saving, and there is none; what the workflow saved was wall-clock time, about sixteen minutes for the three phases against the prompt path's stages 4 to 6, which decision 94 does not count. Spike 3 on this run (decision 101) added the constraint that a resumable engine must call its agents in a fixed order, which gives up the overlap that produced the time saving. So the workflow engine is not built in this pass; it stays specified in requirement 270 for the day the host replays by key or the prompt path's token cost rises. The first attempt at the baseline in this session was denied by the session's auto-mode permission classifier with `[Create Unsafe Agents]` and ran after the owner's yes outside auto mode; two further attempts stopped at the first registry write under the plugin data directory, which headless `acceptEdits` refuses, because the scratch profile then held the house rules block of edit seven and the audit obeyed it, so the run that counts had the block removed, as the 2026-10-15 run had no profile `CLAUDE.md`. Settled 2026-10-22. Reopens: a host release that replays a resumed workflow by agent key rather than call order, or a like-for-like run with a second-opinion set that comes in under the prompt path by more than run-to-run variation.
104. **Area test scripts and a policy, taken.** On Windows with 32 CPUs and Git for Windows the whole suite took 891 s and `npm run lint` 1.8 s, and the primer milestones paid that on every commit. One `node --test` script per test directory makes the cheap path the written one: `test:rules` 11 s, `test:loop` under 1 s, `test:release` 17 s, `test:lint` 212 s and `test:primer` about 420 s before decision 105, `test:audit` 678 s before decision 106; the policy is what the house rules already ask, the checks that cover what was touched before and after a change and the repository's checks before reporting done. CI keeps the whole suite. Settled 2026-10-22. Reopens: never.
105. **Concurrency inside the lint and primer hook suites, taken.** `tests/lint/lint.test.mjs` ran 127 cases serially at a median of 1.6 s, 212 s in all; each copies the clean repository and runs lint once, and the cases are independent. `tests/primer/hook.test.mjs` ran 114 tests in 399 s at a median of 3.9 s per timed case; each case that calls `make()` makes its own repository, and only the "files the command itself stages" group shares one. `sh -c :` costs 197 ms and `git --version` 68 ms per spawn here, so both suites are spawn-bound and the gain is parallel spawns, not less work. `tests/rules/rules.test.mjs` at 11 s and `tests/primer/notice.test.mjs` at 15 s are left serial; the gain would not cover the change. The after numbers: `npm test` took 940 s against 891 s before, and back to back 579 s against 532 s for the commit before; alternating runs took 804 s and 1030 s with the concurrency and 1039 s and 1012 s without, on a loaded machine, so the concurrency is not the cause of the slower suite, and `readonly.sh` alone sets the floor. Later runs are acceptance item 44's result. Settled 2026-10-22. Reopens: a CI run on Windows where parallel `git` processes on separate repositories fail, which would set the concurrency lower, never to one.
106. **`revert-tests.sh` reads a tick, taken.** `tests/audit/revert-tests.sh` took 310 s alone and 526 s inside the suite on that Windows machine, against 84 s alone on a Linux container with 2 CPUs; the script under test sleeps one second per poll, two between `wait` checks and ten between TERM and KILL, and the cases prove timeouts with `sleep 60` fixtures the watchdog ends, so about 75 s of either number is the script's own sleeping and the rest here is process creation. A tick variable beside `REVERT_TESTS_CAP`, `REVERT_TESTS_DEADLINE` and `REVERT_TESTS_WAIT` takes the sleeping out of the suite and leaves the shipped timing unchanged; fractional `sleep` works under Git for Windows and macOS, and the mawk job runs awk only. On Linux CI this file is the slowest and the saving is most of it. Settled 2026-10-22. Reopens: a platform whose `sleep` refuses a fraction, which would set the suite's tick to 1 there and nothing else.
107. **Lint's concurrent syntax check was already in the code; declined as a work item, kept as requirement 278.** The pass expected `node --check` to run per file in sequence; `tools/lint.mjs` starts all 17 with `Promise.all`, and a timed run on 2026-10-22 showed them summing to 1,988 ms of process time inside a 1,252 ms lint run. What remains in lint is 17 serial `git` reads at 842 ms, most of them the tag rule's three per plugin; they are not made concurrent, since decision 105 takes their cost out of the suite and a lint run of 1.3 s is not what the owner waits for. Settled 2026-10-22. Reopens: lint above three seconds alone, which would make the `git` reads concurrent.
108. **Declined, with the reason.** One repository copy across the lint cases: each case mutates its copy, and sharing would make the order matter. A Node rewrite of the audit sh suites: decision 12 and requirement 6 keep the scripts in sh with their suites in sh, and the cost here is process creation, which a rewrite of the suites alone would not remove. Dropping cases from the Windows run: requirement 17 runs every test on every platform; the two `working-tree.sh` cases that skip there already skip for a file system reason, not for time. Settled 2026-10-22. Reopens: never.
109. **Auto mode needs no rule change.** Items 40 to 42 ran on 2.1.304 headless under `--permission-mode auto`. Item 40's full run published with no denial, and with ask-first rules the loop asked before the commit, the push and the pull request, as the house rules say, with `permission_denials` empty, and the audit's low tier reached `reported` with all six read-only checks passing and no write. The one denial, in item 42, came from the host's built-in removal check and not from the classifier: the run built the registry lock path from shell variables, `rmdir $L/$O $L`, which `SKILL.md` already forbids ("always written as a literal absolute path, never built from a shell variable"), and its reissue with literal paths ran. No manual-mode run showed that denial; the rule that prevents it is already in the skill, so the user meets it only when a run departs from the skill, and the run recovered on its own. Settled 2026-10-22. Reopens: an auto-mode denial of a call the skills prescribe.
110. **The marketplace and repository are renamed to `reimagine-engine`.** The catalog name, the `package.json` name, the four manifests' repository URL, the tracked project settings, the README install lines, the lint checks and the design documents now say `reimagine-engine`; the GitHub repository is renamed the same day and the old URL redirects. The plugin ids `rules`, `loop`, `audit` and `primer` do not change, so skill names, hooks, the output style, tag names and the "reimagine audit" and "reimagine loop" triggers stay as they are. The catalog's `metadata.version` and the four plugin versions move by a patch, the plugins because requirement 14 wants a version above the last tag once a plugin directory changes, and the URL and install lines are such a change. Acceptance entries record their runs under the old name and are not rewritten; the lead of `docs/acceptance.md` says so. Settled 2026-10-23. Reopens: a second user who installed under the old name, since their `enabledPlugins` keys and marketplace entry would need the new name.
111. **House-rule edit nine and Concise Plain 4.6.** Field feedback on rules 0.1.5 found five cases of the model skipping a rule the style or the house rules already state; each gets one line and, where it fits, a check step. The five: a report said a rule was reworded, "me" to "the user", when the files held it word for word; a report paraphrased a person's words in a way that changed their meaning; drafts for pasting into chat wrote ticket ids bare, in two sessions, while the same sessions linked them in replies to the user; drafts to others restated the ticket comment in full, four corrections in three sessions; and the casual register meant for replies to the user leaked into drafts to others, two corrections. The first is edit nine, requirement 280, placed in the house rules under Done because it governs how every session reports an edit, whatever output style is active; placement item 11 of requirement 241 puts a fact true of every project there. The other four govern prose, so they go in the style and the chat block, which become Concise Plain 4.6: a Content bullet after "Prose over a table" that reports a person's words or a paraphrase that adds nothing, names the options as they were put and labels a reading as the writer's; a Drafts bullet after "Write for the least technical person" that admits a fact only when the reader cannot get it from a source the draft names and keeps a chat reply to a few sentences; the last Drafts bullet gains a check step, a scan for bare ids of the kinds the Links section names before the draft is shown; and the first Tone bullet makes a draft to someone else match its reader, plain and professional by default. The block's list of deliberate differences does not change, since each new line differs from the style in nothing. The feedback also proposed a hook that would deny a reply holding a bare id; it is not built: a reply that quotes an id from a ticket title, a commit message or the user's own words is a false positive, and lint pins the two `hooks.json` shapes of requirement 219, so a third hook changes lint as well as the plugin. The upgrade-diff item of the same feedback was fixed in 0.1.4 and needs nothing. This is not the ninth edit the reopen clause of decision 93 anticipated; the two items it names are not in tension. Settled 2026-10-24 by the owner. Reopens: the Drafts line on links inside a draft (the principle sentence since decision 158) not holding in the field, which would make the hook worth its false positives.
112. **Field feedback on loop 0.1.4 and audit 0.1.6: eight findings built, one dropped, the record helper deferred.** Reports from 2026-10-23 and 2026-10-24 (Windows, Claude Code 2.1.309) were verified on 2026-10-24 against the shipped texts, which equal the tagged versions except for the rename lines of decision 110; the texts already carry loop 0.1.5 and audit 0.1.7 from the rename commit, untagged, so no version moves. The findings and what each rests on: (1) the loop's primary checkout could not be named, since no request phrase set it and Step 0.2.3's two ways around a branch checked out in another worktree were to start the run inside it or to make it an additional repository; in a subagent, a Bash `cd` and a PowerShell `Set-Location` issued as their own calls did not survive to the next call in either tool (six calls, 2026-10-24), so "start the run inside it" is not available from one. The phrase is "primary <path>" (requirement 281), mirroring the Scope line the reading block already prints; "in <path>" was rejected because the remaining request text is joined into the ad-hoc description and "in" occurs in prose. With a named checkout that is a linked worktree, the record lives under its main checkout, as the detached run of `worktree.md` already keeps it and as the audit's scratch rule of requirement 175 expects, and the detached-worktree run does not apply: the named checkout is the worktree, so a differing flagged path there is a new stop that names the main checkout as the primary to run with the exception. (2) No base branch but the remote default, while `handoff.md` and the audit manifest already carried `base <remote>/<base branch>`; "base <branch>" and the `.reimagine.json` key `base` (requirement 282), the phrase winning, and the symbol `<default>` of Step 7 becomes `<base>` wherever it meant the review or pull request base, which moves one word each in requirements 56 and 125, since a `pull_request_target` workflow runs from the base ref; "against <base>" was rejected because the audit's grammar uses "against" for a branch bundle and the two would read alike for different things. (3) The loop's `decided_by` mapping sent `run` to `not recorded`, which drops a recorded fact, and the audit's `handoff.md` carried no mapping while plugins install alone; both now carry one table, `run` mapping to `role: orchestrator`, the role word the owner picked, which `handoff.sh` accepts as any non-empty role (requirement 139). (4) Stage 8 named no tool for the body write, and a run wrote it by shell heredoc; on 2026-10-24 on Windows with Node 26.4.0, `spawnSync('bash', ['-c', <heredoc>])` exited 0 at 16 KB and 31 KB of command text and failed with `ENAMETOOLONG` at 33 KB and 66 KB, the process argument cap, so the body is written with the Write tool and only the revision line and the assembly of `report.md` run by shell, as `claims-verdicts.md` already said. (5) The run copies of `common.md` and `audit-evidence.md` crossed the context twice per run, about 900 lines, and a transcription slip would govern the run; `run-copies.sh` (requirement 283) fills the header with awk and copies the evidence file unchanged, the tenth script, with the question list written by the orchestrator to `questions.md` first so the script holds no copy of the four defaults. (6) Stage 7 chose split mode on the sum of the ledger sizes; a run with eleven small groups and a 462,000-byte sum launched twelve mergers whose largest slice was about 60,000 bytes, while decision 70 keeps the threshold to size one merger's context, which the sum does not measure; the slices are cut first and split mode is taken when a slice passes the threshold or the sum passes three times it (requirement 159), and `_test.ledger_split_bytes` now bounds a slice; the item 14 rerun of 2026-10-24 skipped the cut at a 75,544-byte sum and said so in its entry, and since no slice can pass a threshold the sum is under, the cut is skipped there and the largest slice recorded as `not cut`, settled the same day. (7) Act's close named `live.md` as a run file; it is the skill document, and the blocks are in `report.md`. (8) Act asked again for every rewrite of a regenerated file, since a yes covered one saved diff and a file whose regenerated bytes differ on every build, such as a generated test file whose ids change on every build, differs on every check; the first yes now covers the path for the invocation (requirement 193), and "without a yes, stop" stays, since it is an ask-first rule. Dropped: the read-only check past the two-minute shell limit on a large build's output was not reproduced; on 2026-10-24 `readonly.sh snapshot` over a repository with 30,000 ignored files took 9 s and `readonly.sh check` after all 30,000 were rewritten took 14 s with 30,000 `ignored changed` lines and exit 3 (Windows, 32 CPUs); the per-file output is real and a per-folder summary is a note for a later pass. Deferred: the record helper for `steps.json` and `stages.json`. Decision 31 reopens it on a field run where hand bookkeeping slipped; the report counts more than twenty hand edits and no slip, the audit scripts are POSIX sh plus awk and editing JSON in awk is a new class of script, and the loop ships no script (requirement 6). A slip, or a third report of the count, builds `stages.sh` for the audit alone in its own pull request. Settled 2026-10-24 by the owner. Reopens: a field run where a named linked worktree needs the detached run too, which would lift its flagged-path stop; a run that needs a review base other than the pull request base, which would split the phrase in two; a reproduced read-only check past two minutes, which would build the per-folder summary; and the record helper as stated.
113. **House-rule edit ten and Concise Plain 4.7: the principles stay, the sentences with no failure behind them go.** A two-pass review on 2026-10-24 of the edits since the agreed text, requirements 232 to 236, 269 and 280 and Concise Plain 4.6, the second pass challenging the first, found two kinds of sentence.
